The discrete nature of our latent variables facilitates search-based strategies and provides a foundation for future applications of reinforcement learning to dialogue systems, where learning can occur at the state level rather than the token level.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "SAGE: Steering and Refining Dialog Generation with State-Action Augmentation":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces SAGE (State Augmented GEneration), a novel approach to building emotionally intelligent chatbots.  SAGE uses a State-Action Chain (SAC) to augment language model fine-tuning. The SAC introduces latent variables representing emotional states and conversational strategies between dialogue turns. During inference, these variables are generated before each response, enabling higher-level control over dialogue progression while maintaining naturalness. The paper also presents a self-improvement pipeline using dialogue tree search, LLM-based reward modeling, and targeted fine-tuning to optimize conversational trajectories.  Experiments show that models trained with SAGE improve emotional intelligence metrics without sacrificing performance on standard LLM benchmarks.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of using latent variables to control high-level conversational aspects like emotional state and strategy *is* novel in the context of chatbot fine-tuning. Augmenting the training data with a "State-Action Chain" to explicitly model these latent variables is also a creative contribution.  The self-improvement pipeline, while building upon existing techniques like dialogue tree search and LLM-based reward modeling, integrates them in a specific way tailored for the SAGE framework. However, it should be noted that techniques like latent variable modeling, self-play and LLM-based rewards are individually not novel, but it is the combination and application to emotional chatbots that offers some originality.
*   **Significance:**  Emotionally intelligent chatbots are an increasingly important area. SAGE's ability to steer dialogue and refine its emotional response has potential for creating more engaging and helpful AI companions.  The discrete nature of the latent variables is significant because it facilitates using search-based strategies and lays the groundwork for reinforcement learning applied at the state level rather than the token level.  The results, showing improvement in emotional intelligence metrics while maintaining LLM benchmark performance, indicate a practical advantage.
*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition: The paper clearly identifies the challenge of building emotionally intelligent chatbots.
    *   Technically sound approach: SAGE is well-defined and implemented using established techniques like fine-tuning and tree search.
    *   Experimental validation:  The paper presents quantitative and qualitative results to support its claims. The comparison against a baseline *without* SAC augmentation is particularly important.
    *   Potential for future work: The framework is designed to be compatible with RL, which opens promising research directions.
*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Reliance on LLM-based evaluation: While LLM-based evaluation has become more prevalent, it may not perfectly align with human preferences, especially concerning nuances of emotion and conversational quality.  More human evaluation would strengthen the results.
    *   Performance Degradation on Technical Tasks: The reported performance decline on GSM8k, for instance, is a potential concern, indicating a possible trade-off between emotional intelligence and problem-solving ability.  Further investigation into how to mitigate this degradation is needed.  The authors do suggest mitigating this by incorporating instruction-tuned datasets, such as ShareGPT data, into the training process.
    *   Limited Generalizability: The framework might be heavily tuned to the specific in-house dataset and LLM (Mixtral). Showing its adaptability to other datasets and model architectures would enhance the impact.
    *   The "incarnation" issue where the chatbot pretends to be human is concerning.
*   **Impact:** SAGE offers a compelling framework for advancing the field of emotionally intelligent chatbots. The idea of state augmented generation can inspire other works to explore explicit modeling of conversational dynamics.

**Rigorous Rationale:**

While SAGE is a valuable contribution, the weaknesses prevent a truly exceptional score. The reliance on LLM-based evaluation, the performance degradation on tasks like GSM8k, and the potential for over-specialization limit the impact. Furthermore, while the combination of techniques is clever, the individual components are well-established. The hallucination of being a human presents a significant problem.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Learning from Noisy Labels with Contrastive Co-Transformer
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03042v1)
- **Authors**: Yan Han, Soumava Kumar Roy, Mehrtash Harandi, Lars Petersson
- **Abstract**: Deep learning with noisy labels is an interesting challenge in weakly supervised learning. Despite their significant learning capacity, CNNs have a tendency to overfit in the presence of samples with noisy labels. Alleviating this issue, the well known Co-Training framework is used as a fundamental basis for our work. In this paper, we introduce a Contrastive Co-Transformer framework, which is simple and fast, yet able to improve the performance by a large margin compared to the state-of-the-art approaches. We argue the robustness of transformers when dealing with label noise. Our Contrastive Co-Transformer approach is able to utilize all samples in the dataset, irrespective of whether they are clean or noisy. Transformers are trained by a combination of contrastive loss and classification loss. Extensive experimental results on corrupted data from six standard benchmark datasets including Clothing1M, demonstrate that our Contrastive Co-Transformer is superior to existing state-of-the-art methods.
- **Summary**: Okay, here's a summary and a critical evaluation of the paper "Learning from Noisy Labels with Contrastive Co-Transformer":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a new method, Contrastive Co-Transformer (CCT), to improve the robustness of deep learning models when trained on datasets with noisy labels. CCT builds upon the Co-Training framework, employing two homogeneous transformer networks. It integrates a contrastive loss module to enhance the learning of distinct but complementary features by the two transformers.  Unlike standard Co-Training methods that select "clean" samples based on loss values, CCT utilizes all samples in the mini-batch, regardless of their presumed label accuracy, within the contrastive loss calculation. This approach is designed to leverage both supervised and unsupervised learning aspects. The authors evaluate CCT on several benchmark datasets, demonstrating improved performance compared to state-of-the-art noisy label learning methods, particularly with high noise rates.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in the specific combination of components:
    *   Applying transformers within a Co-Training framework for noisy label learning is not entirely new, but the authors convincingly show the effectiveness of this approach compared to CNN-based co-training.
    *   The crucial contribution is the contrastive loss module. While contrastive learning itself is well-established, its integration within the Co-Training framework, using the *outputs of the co-transformers themselves as positive pairs*, is a genuinely novel element. It circumvents the need for dataset-specific data augmentation strategies to create positive pairs, simplifying the overall pipeline and making it more generalizable.
    *   The paper convincingly argues that *using all the samples* in the mini-batch (both deemed "clean" and "noisy" by the co-training framework) during the constrastive loss computation leads to improved performance, providing a more robust training procedure.

*   **Significance:** The significance of the paper depends on whether the proposed method delivers meaningful performance improvements across various scenarios. The experimental results, showing CCT outperforming existing approaches on several datasets and noise levels, provide empirical support for its effectiveness. The improvement is most pronounced in datasets with high noise rates where other methods falter, which is particularly relevant given the increasing reliance on weakly supervised data.
    *   The simplicity and generality of the method (avoiding complex data augmentation schemes) also add to its significance, as it could be easily adopted and applied in various real-world scenarios with noisy labels.
    * The ability to use a transformer within this structure is also meaningful, as it will enable better modelling and understanding of datasets that have dependencies between the labels.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem statement and motivation.
    *   Well-explained methodology with a clear description of the CCT architecture and the contrastive loss integration.
    *   Comprehensive experimental evaluation on multiple datasets with varying noise levels.
    *   Demonstrated superior performance compared to several state-of-the-art methods.
    *   The use of all samples for contrastive loss, rather than just selected clean samples, is a valuable contribution.
    *   Relatively straightforward implementation and integration.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   While the experimental results are strong, a deeper analysis of why CCT works so well compared to existing methods could strengthen the paper. For instance, visualizing the feature space learned by CCT and comparing it to other methods could offer further insights.
    *   The hyperparameter λ (weighting the contrastive loss) is fixed across all experiments. A sensitivity analysis of λ could further improve the robustness of the method.
    *   The paper could benefit from more detailed ablation studies, such as exploring the impact of different contrastive loss functions.
    *   The paper could benefit from experiments where the noise rate is *not* assumed to be known and needs to be estimated.

*   **Potential Influence:**
    *   CCT could become a go-to method for learning from noisy labels, especially in scenarios with high noise rates.
    *   The idea of using co-transformer outputs as positive pairs for contrastive learning could inspire further research in combining self-supervised and supervised learning techniques within the Co-Training framework.
    *   The general principle of leveraging all samples (including noisy ones) in a contrastive manner may have broader applicability beyond the noisy label problem.

**Justification for Score:**

Despite some minor weaknesses, the paper presents a novel and effective approach to learning from noisy labels. The combination of transformers, Co-Training, and the contrastive loss module (using co-transformer outputs as positive pairs) is innovative and well-motivated. The experimental results are compelling, demonstrating superior performance across multiple datasets and noise levels. The method's simplicity and generality further enhance its potential impact.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 8/10

### Improving LLM-as-a-Judge Inference with the Judgment Distribution
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03064v1)
- **Authors**: Victor Wang, Michael J. Q. Zhang, Eunsol Choi
- **Abstract**: Using language models to scalably approximate human preferences on text quality (LLM-as-a-judge) has become a standard practice applicable to many tasks. A judgment is often extracted from the judge's textual output alone, typically with greedy decoding. However, LLM judges naturally provide distributions over judgment tokens, inviting a breadth of inference methods for extracting fine-grained preferences. We find that taking the mean of the judgment distribution consistently outperforms taking the mode (i.e. greedy decoding) in all evaluation settings (i.e. pointwise, pairwise, and listwise). We further explore novel methods of deriving preferences from judgment distributions, and find that methods incorporating risk aversion often improve performance. Lastly, we analyze LLM-as-a-judge paired with chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting, showing that CoT can collapse the spread of the judgment distribution, often harming performance. Our findings suggest leveraging distributional output can improve LLM-as-a-judge, as opposed to using the text interface alone.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper "Improving LLM-as-a-Judge Inference with the Judgment Distribution" addresses the prevalent practice of using Large Language Models (LLMs) as judges for evaluating text quality, but highlights that current approaches predominantly rely on the LLM's textual output alone, typically obtained through greedy decoding. It argues that LLMs inherently provide probability distributions over judgment tokens, which can be leveraged for more fine-grained preference extraction. The authors comprehensively evaluate different methods for extracting preferences from these judgment distributions, comparing mode (greedy decoding) against mean and other novel techniques like risk-averse measures. They examine these methods across pointwise, pairwise, and listwise evaluation settings. Furthermore, the paper analyzes the impact of chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting on LLM-as-a-judge performance, finding that CoT can sometimes harm performance by collapsing the judgment distribution. The authors advocate for leveraging the distributional output of LLMs as judges, contrasting this with the common practice of relying solely on the text interface.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's primary novelty lies in its systematic exploration of using LLM judgment *distributions*, rather than just point estimates from textual outputs (like the mode). While a few previous works hinted at the benefits of using the mean, this paper conducts a much more thorough empirical comparison and explores novel methods of incorporating the entire distribution. The observation regarding CoT's potentially detrimental effect on judgment distribution and the subsequent analysis is also a significant contribution.

*   **Significance:**  LLM-as-a-judge is a rapidly growing field with broad applications in evaluating text quality, aligning language models, and performing reinforcement learning from AI feedback (RLAIF). Improving the efficiency and accuracy of LLM judges is therefore highly impactful.  The finding that leveraging judgment distributions improves performance, often significantly, could change the standard practices in this field.  The analysis of CoT's trade-offs provides valuable insights for prompt engineering.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Comprehensive Empirical Evaluation:** The paper presents a wide range of experiments across different datasets (RewardBench, MT-Bench), LLMs (GPT-40, Llama-3.1-8B, Mistral-7B, Prometheus-2-7B), and evaluation settings (pointwise, pairwise, listwise). This thoroughness strengthens the generalizability of the findings.
    *   **Analysis of CoT Prompting:**  The discovery that CoT is not always beneficial and can sometimes hinder LLM-as-a-judge is a valuable and counterintuitive finding. The paper offers a plausible explanation for this phenomenon (sharpening of the distribution) and provides empirical evidence to support it.
    *   **Exploration of Risk Aversion:** Investigating methods that incorporate risk aversion in judgment extraction is a novel and promising direction.
    *   **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written, clearly explains the methods, experimental setup, and results. The inclusion of tables summarizing the results is highly useful.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited exploration of other scoring methods.** Although it is not the primary goal of this paper, the paper introduces and investigates other ways of extracting value from LLM distributions. However, there exist more scoring techniques, such as methods for dealing with distributional reward, that the paper leaves for future research.
    *   **Lack of qualitative analysis:** While the quantitative results are convincing, a qualitative analysis of example judgments and their distributions would further enhance the paper's insights.  For example, showing how the mean captures nuanced preferences in cases where the mode fails would be valuable.
    *   **Limited downstream evaluation:** The paper focuses primarily on performance on preference modeling datasets. A downstream evaluation demonstrating the impact of these improved LLM judges on tasks like RLAIF or human decision making would strengthen its significance.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to significantly influence the LLM-as-a-judge research community and practitioners. Its findings could lead to a shift away from relying solely on text-based judgments and towards leveraging judgment distributions. The insights regarding CoT and the effectiveness of mean-based approaches are directly actionable and can improve the performance of LLM judges in various applications.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the paper's novelty, comprehensive evaluation, and potential for impact, but considering the weaknesses such as a lack of qualitative analysis and limited exploration of downstream applications, a score in the upper-middle range is justified.

**Score: 7.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Multi-View Depth Consistent Image Generation Using Generative AI Models: Application on Architectural Design of University Buildings
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03068v1)
- **Authors**: Xusheng Du, Ruihan Gui, Zhengyang Wang, Ye Zhang, Haoran Xie
- **Abstract**: In the early stages of architectural design, shoebox models are typically used as a simplified representation of building structures but require extensive operations to transform them into detailed designs. Generative artificial intelligence (AI) provides a promising solution to automate this transformation, but ensuring multi-view consistency remains a significant challenge. To solve this issue, we propose a novel three-stage consistent image generation framework using generative AI models to generate architectural designs from shoebox model representations. The proposed method enhances state-of-the-art image generation diffusion models to generate multi-view consistent architectural images. We employ ControlNet as the backbone and optimize it to accommodate multi-view inputs of architectural shoebox models captured from predefined perspectives. To ensure stylistic and structural consistency across multi-view images, we propose an image space loss module that incorporates style loss, structural loss and angle alignment loss. We then use depth estimation method to extract depth maps from the generated multi-view images. Finally, we use the paired data of the architectural images and depth maps as inputs to improve the multi-view consistency via the depth-aware 3D attention module. Experimental results demonstrate that the proposed framework can generate multi-view architectural images with consistent style and structural coherence from shoebox model inputs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a three-stage generative AI framework designed to create multi-view consistent architectural images from simplified "shoebox" models. The method leverages ControlNet for image generation, incorporating a multi-view module for processing multiple perspectives simultaneously. An image-space loss module, including style, structural, and angle alignment losses, ensures consistency across views. Finally, depth estimation and a depth-aware 3D attention module further refine the multi-view coherence. The framework is demonstrated on the task of generating architectural designs of university buildings, showing improved consistency and structural coherence compared to existing methods.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in its combination of existing techniques (ControlNet, depth estimation, multi-view diffusion) into a tailored framework for generating multi-view consistent architectural images from simple shoebox models. While each component is individually established, their integration and application to architectural design is a specific contribution. The proposed image space loss module, incorporating style, structural, and angle alignment losses, adds some incremental novelty for cross-view consistency. However, the concepts of style and structural losses are broadly known, and the specific implementation may represent a refinement rather than a groundbreaking innovation.

*   **Significance:** The potential significance resides in automating the transformation from initial conceptual designs (shoebox models) to detailed visual renderings, streamlining the architectural design process. The focus on multi-view consistency is crucial for ensuring accurate spatial representation and reducing design errors. However, the experimental evaluation is somewhat limited. The use of a proprietary dataset restricts independent verification, and the qualitative results, while promising, lack a more rigorous quantitative assessment. The user study, while valuable, might not fully capture the practical utility for professional architects in real-world design scenarios. Furthermore, the reliance on simple shoebox models, as acknowledged by the authors, poses a limitation. While it simplifies the initial stage, it may not effectively address the complexities of more intricate and diverse architectural designs.

*   **Strengths:** The paper offers a well-structured approach to a specific problem. The integration of ControlNet and depth information is logical and relevant. The user study provides some evidence of the framework's perceived quality. The paper provides a good background of related research, and clearly states the aim and the key methods utilized in the study.
*   **Weaknesses:** The limitations include:
    *   The novelty might be incremental rather than revolutionary.
    *   The dataset used is specific to a particular type of building and is not publicly accessible, limiting the generalizability and reproducibility of the findings.
    *   The complexity of real-world architectural inputs is not fully captured.
    *   Quantitative evaluation is limited, relying primarily on qualitative results and a user study.

**Justification for Score:**

Given the above analysis, a score of 6 seems appropriate. The paper presents a novel integration of existing techniques, resulting in a functional framework for a relevant application. However, the incremental novelty of the individual components and limitations in dataset diversity and evaluation prevent a higher score.

**Score: 6**

- **Classification**: cs.GR
- **Score**: 6/10

### BEVDriver: Leveraging BEV Maps in LLMs for Robust Closed-Loop Driving
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03074v1)
- **Authors**: Katharina Winter, Mark Azer, Fabian B. Flohr
- **Abstract**: Autonomous driving has the potential to set the stage for more efficient future mobility, requiring the research domain to establish trust through safe, reliable and transparent driving. Large Language Models (LLMs) possess reasoning capabilities and natural language understanding, presenting the potential to serve as generalized decision-makers for ego-motion planning that can interact with humans and navigate environments designed for human drivers. While this research avenue is promising, current autonomous driving approaches are challenged by combining 3D spatial grounding and the reasoning and language capabilities of LLMs. We introduce BEVDriver, an LLM-based model for end-to-end closed-loop driving in CARLA that utilizes latent BEV features as perception input. BEVDriver includes a BEV encoder to efficiently process multi-view images and 3D LiDAR point clouds. Within a common latent space, the BEV features are propagated through a Q-Former to align with natural language instructions and passed to the LLM that predicts and plans precise future trajectories while considering navigation instructions and critical scenarios. On the LangAuto benchmark, our model reaches up to 18.9% higher performance on the Driving Score compared to SoTA methods.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the BEVDriver paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces BEVDriver, an end-to-end motion planner for autonomous driving using Large Language Models (LLMs). It leverages Bird's-Eye-View (BEV) feature maps, fused from LiDAR and camera data, as input to the LLM, enabling the model to predict trajectories and plan maneuvers in response to natural language navigation instructions. The BEV features are aligned with language instructions using a Q-Former. The model is tested in the CARLA simulator and shows state-of-the-art performance on the LangAuto benchmark, surpassing existing methods in driving score and route completion. A key aspect is the elimination of pre-predicted waypoint tokens as LLM input, enabling a more direct interpretation of high-dimensional BEV features.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty lies primarily in the architecture that integrates BEV features directly into an LLM for end-to-end driving, removing the reliance on pre-predicted waypoints. Combining BEV maps with LLMs has been explored before (e.g., Talk2BEV, LMDrive), but BEVDriver presents a more direct approach that allows the LLM to directly interpret high-dimensional features without relying on pre-conditioned, trajectory-specific processing. The use of a Q-Former for feature alignment is a standard practice. The end-to-end training approach also sets it apart from methods using LLMs solely for high-level planning or waypoint refinement.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from improved performance on the LangAuto benchmark, a standard for evaluating language-guided autonomous driving. The reported gains of up to 18.9% over state-of-the-art in Driving Score are substantial and suggest a meaningful advancement. By addressing the limitations of absolute spatial understanding in LLMs for driving and removing the intermediate waypoint prediction, the paper contributes to closing the gap between LLM reasoning and precise motion planning. The open-source release of the code and dataset is another significant contribution, facilitating further research and development. The paper does include ablation studies, including a Town 5 evaluation, but more ablation studies are needed to ensure robust training.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   Clear presentation of the architecture and method.
    *   Demonstrated performance improvement on a recognized benchmark.
    *   Emphasis on end-to-end learning and direct BEV feature interpretation.
    *   Open-source release to promote reproducibility and further research.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   Limited discussion of the computational cost and scalability of the model.
    *   Lack of real-world validation or discussion of potential challenges in translating the method to real-world scenarios.
    *   The Llama3.1-8B-Instruct model performed worse than the Llama-7b model, so the model should have been trained on a more comprehensive parameter set.
    *   The paper needs more ablation studies to ensure the training is robust.

*   **Potential Influence:** The paper has the potential to influence future research in LLM-based autonomous driving by demonstrating the effectiveness of direct BEV feature integration. The performance gains and open-source release could lead to the adoption of similar architectures and training strategies in other systems. Future research may focus on addressing the limitations of the BEVDriver architecture, such as improving its temporal reasoning and extending it to more complex and realistic driving scenarios.

**Rigorous Rationale for Score:**

The paper makes a significant contribution by directly integrating BEV features into an LLM for autonomous driving, surpassing existing methods on the LangAuto benchmark. Although previous work combined BEV maps and LLMs (e.g., Talk2BEV and LMDrive), BEVDriver enables the LLM to interpret high-dimensional features directly. While it presents a clear architectural method, the model's computational scalability is not clearly discussed. Overall, it presents a meaningful advancement in end-to-end learning within the realm of autonomous driving. Therefore:

**Score: 7**

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### From Architectural Sketch to Conceptual Representation: Using Structure-Aware Diffusion Model to Generate Renderings of School Buildings
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03090v1)
- **Authors**: Zhengyang Wang, Hao Jin, Xusheng Du, Yuxiao Ren, Ye Zhang, Haoran Xie
- **Abstract**: Generative Artificial Intelligence (AI) has advanced rapidly, enabling the generation of renderings from architectural sketches. This progress has significantly improved the efficiency of communication and conceptual expression during the early stage of architectural design. However, generated images often lack the structural details from architects' sketches. While sketches typically emphasize the overall structure, crucial components such as windows and doors are often represented by simple lines or omitted entirely. For school buildings, it is essential to control architectural components, such as the shape and proportion of windows, as these factors directly influence the accuracy of the generated images in reflecting the architect's design intentions. To address this issue, we propose a structure-aware diffusion model for architectural image generation to refine expressing design intentions through retrieval augmentation. Our framework utilizes architectural components to enhance the generation process, addressing the details that may be lacking in the sketches. These components provide clear spatial and structural details, improving the model's ability to interpret and generate architectural details. The refined sketches, combined with text prompts, are fed into the proposed structure-aware diffusion model to generate detailed and realistic school building images. The experiment results demonstrate the effectiveness of our framework in generating architectural designs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces a structure-aware diffusion model for generating architectural renderings of school buildings from sketches. The core idea is to enhance the generative process by incorporating architectural components retrieved from a dataset, addressing the common issue of simplified or missing details in initial sketches (especially regarding windows and doors). The proposed framework involves architectural component extraction (using SAM), retrieval augmentation (using sketch-based image retrieval and LDM for refinement), and a structure-aware generation process that utilizes a diffusion model conditioned on both text prompts and the detailed sketch. The authors present results on a custom-built dataset, comparing their method against state-of-the-art diffusion models (Stable Diffusion, SDXL, ControlNet) through quantitative metrics (PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS) and a user study.

**Critical Evaluation:**

* **Strengths:**
    * **Problem Relevance:** Addressing the challenge of incorporating structural detail from sketches into generative AI models for architectural design is a relevant and practical problem.  The focus on school buildings, with their characteristic designs and detailing, allows for targeted improvement.
    * **Methodological Soundness:** The pipeline is well-defined and integrates several existing techniques (SAM, sketch retrieval, diffusion models) in a sensible manner. The retrieval augmentation component helps bridge the gap between simplistic sketches and realistic renderings.  Using LDM for sketch refinement before feeding it to the main generative model is a notable element.
    * **Datasets:**  Creating custom datasets (both for component retrieval and for training) demonstrates a commitment to the task and provides a valuable resource for future work. This addresses the lack of suitable datasets for this specific application.
    * **Evaluation:** The use of quantitative metrics (PSNR, SSIM, LPIPS) provides a baseline evaluation, while the user study offers qualitative insights into user perception.  The comparison against strong baselines (Stable Diffusion, SDXL, ControlNet) strengthens the argument for the proposed method's effectiveness.
    * **Clear Presentation:** The paper is well-written and clearly explains the proposed methodology.

* **Weaknesses:**
    * **Limited Novelty:** The individual components of the framework (SAM, sketch retrieval, diffusion models) are well-established. The primary novelty lies in the specific combination and application to the architectural rendering domain. While the application is practical, the methodological innovation is incremental.
    * **Dataset Scale:** While the creation of custom datasets is a positive aspect, the size of the datasets (300 component images, 1600 sketch-rendering pairs) is relatively modest. Larger datasets might lead to more robust and generalizable results.
    * **Limited Component Scope:** The focus primarily on windows and doors, while understandable, limits the generality of the approach.  Expanding the component library and retrieval system to handle a wider range of architectural elements (e.g., facades, roofs, balconies) would enhance its applicability.
    * **Qualitative Evaluation:**  While the user study provides some indication of user satisfaction, a more in-depth qualitative analysis (e.g., visual comparisons, ablation studies isolating the impact of the retrieval augmentation) could further illuminate the strengths and weaknesses of the approach.  The paper lacks visual examples that clearly showcase the benefits of the architecture component dataset.
    * **Integration Challenges:** The stated limitation regarding seamless integration of replaced components with overall designs should be addressed. The paper highlights that regeneration of local sketch areas has difficulty integrating trees, pillars, and nearby obstructions, making the rendered architecture "appear visually unnatural or inconsistent." A significant contribution would involve a method for overcoming these issues.

* **Significance:**
    * The paper provides a practical workflow for generating architectural renderings from sketches, potentially streamlining the early design phase.
    * The approach addresses a specific limitation of existing generative AI models in architectural design (lack of structural detail).
    * The created datasets contribute to the architectural design research community.
    * The combination of different components is not fundamentally novel, but it works for architecture designs in practice.

**Score Justification:**

The paper presents a valuable and practical application of existing technologies to a relevant problem in architectural design. The method is well-defined, and the experimental results demonstrate its effectiveness compared to state-of-the-art baselines. However, the limited methodological novelty, dataset scale, and component scope, as well as some integration challenges, prevent it from being considered a groundbreaking contribution. While useful, the ideas here feel like a well put-together combination of existing ones and lack critical, independent innovation.

### Monitoring Decoding: Mitigating Hallucination via Evaluating the Factuality of Partial Response during Generation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03106v1)
- **Authors**: Yurui Chang, Bochuan Cao, Lu Lin
- **Abstract**: While large language models have demonstrated exceptional performance across a wide range of tasks, they remain susceptible to hallucinations -- generating plausible yet factually incorrect contents. Existing methods to mitigating such risk often rely on sampling multiple full-length generations, which introduces significant response latency and becomes ineffective when the model consistently produces hallucinated outputs with high confidence. To address these limitations, we introduce Monitoring Decoding (MD), a novel framework that dynamically monitors the generation process and selectively applies in-process interventions, focusing on revising crucial tokens responsible for hallucinations. Instead of waiting until completion of multiple full-length generations, we identify hallucination-prone tokens during generation using a monitor function, and further refine these tokens through a tree-based decoding strategy. This approach ensures an enhanced factual accuracy and coherence in the generated output while maintaining efficiency. Experimental results demonstrate that MD consistently outperforms self-consistency-based approaches in both effectiveness and efficiency, achieving higher factual accuracy while significantly reducing computational overhead.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces "Monitoring Decoding" (MD), a novel framework designed to mitigate hallucinations in large language models (LLMs).  Instead of relying on sampling multiple full-length generations like existing methods (Best-of-N, self-consistency), MD dynamically monitors the generation process token by token and selectively applies in-process interventions when a "hallucination-prone" token is detected.  MD uses a monitor function to assess the factual consistency of intermediate tokens and then refines these tokens using a tree-based decoding strategy.  The paper demonstrates through experiments on TruthfulQA, TriviaQA, NQ-Open, and GSM8K that MD outperforms self-consistency-based approaches in both effectiveness (factual accuracy) and efficiency (computational overhead).

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The core idea of intervening *during* generation based on real-time hallucination detection, rather than *after* multiple complete generations, is a significant departure from existing approaches.  While other decoding strategies exist (DoLA, Integrative Decoding), the combination of a monitor function with tree-based selective resampling of *only* suspected tokens is novel. Tree-based search has been used in speculative decoding, but combining this with hallucination detection as a means of guiding the search space has not been done before. The focus on *token-level* correction is a finer-grained approach than methods that operate on entire sentences or responses.

*   **Significance:** Hallucinations remain a major obstacle to the broader adoption of LLMs in critical applications. MD addresses a key limitation of sampling-based methods: inefficiency and ineffectiveness when the model is confidently wrong. If MD proves robust and scalable, it could significantly reduce the computational cost of ensuring factual accuracy, making LLMs more reliable in various contexts. The results in the paper support the claim that the authors framework reduces computational overhead while improving factual accuracy.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Clear Problem Definition:** The paper clearly articulates the limitations of existing methods in mitigating hallucinations, particularly the inefficiency of resampling full-length generations.
    *   **Well-Defined Framework:** MD is presented as a well-defined and modular framework with clear components (monitor function, tree-based decoding).
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The evaluation is conducted across multiple datasets (TruthfulQA, TriviaQA, NQ-Open, GSM8K) and models (Llama-2, Llama-3, and Gemma-2), demonstrating the general applicability of MD.
    *   **Performance Gains:** The experimental results show substantial improvements in factual accuracy compared to baseline methods. The improvements are most prominent on GSM8K, showing its potential on tasks involving reasoning where hallucinations are particularly detrimental.
    *   **Efficiency Results:** The latency and throughput comparisons explicitly show that MD has a significant gain in speed over other methods that re-sample, solidifying the claim that the method improves efficiency.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Complexity:** The tree-based decoding strategy could introduce additional complexity in implementation and tuning, especially regarding the parameters K and N, where the benefits are only observed to a certain point.
    *   **Monitor Function Reliance:** The effectiveness of MD is heavily dependent on the quality of the monitor function (i.e., the reference model). The paper doesn't deeply explore the limitations or biases of the monitor function and how it might affect the overall performance of MD. While larger models are used, those models might exhibit biases or may not be capable of accurately identifying hallucinations.
    *   **Limited Ablation on Parameters:** The ablation study provides only an analysis on the impact of varying the number of samples and the thresholding parameters, and the experiments only uses 3 different models to assess its robustness, which may limit insights into the impact of the tree-based decoding (parameters N and K) specifically. Furthermore, there is no ablation on which portion of the method is important, e.g., what if the monitor function is removed?
    *   **Generalizability:** The paper primarily uses question-answering and reasoning tasks. Testing MD on other tasks like summarization or dialogue generation would further strengthen its generalizability claim.
    *   **Black-box Nature:** The monitor function uses a larger LLM (black-box). It is unclear what factors of those models contribute to their hallucination-mitigating capabilities. Thus, there is a lack of explainability for the detection mechanism.

*   **Potential Influence:** If the efficiency and effectiveness of MD hold up at scale and across diverse tasks, it could become a valuable technique for improving the reliability of LLMs. It could also influence the development of more robust hallucination detection methods.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper demonstrates a significant innovation in addressing a critical problem in the field of LLMs. The framework is well-defined, and the experimental results are promising. The limitations, particularly the dependence on the monitor function and lack of deeper ablation studies, prevent a higher score. Also, I believe that more exploration to understand the trade-offs between accuracy, latency, and search depth will also significantly improve the contribution.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### SoK: Knowledge is All You Need: Last Mile Delivery for Automated Provenance-based Intrusion Detection with LLMs
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03108v1)
- **Authors**: Wenrui Cheng, Tiantian Zhu, Chunlin Xiong, Haofei Sun, Zijun Wang, Shunan Jing, Mingqi Lv, Yan Chen
- **Abstract**: Recently, provenance-based intrusion detection systems (PIDSes) have been widely proposed for endpoint threat analysis. However, due to the lack of systematic integration and utilization of knowledge, existing PIDSes still require significant manual intervention for practical deployment, making full automation challenging. This paper presents a disruptive innovation by categorizing PIDSes according to the types of knowledge they utilize. In response to the prevalent issue of ``knowledge silos problem'' in existing research, we introduce a novel knowledge-driven provenance-based intrusion detection framework, powered by large language models (LLMs). We also present OmniSec, a best practice system built upon this framework. By integrating attack representation knowledge, threat intelligence knowledge, and benign behavior knowledge, OmniSec outperforms the state-of-the-art approaches on public benchmark datasets. OmniSec is available online at https://anonymous.4open.science/r/PIDS-with-LLM-613B.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper proposes a novel, knowledge-driven framework for provenance-based intrusion detection systems (PIDSes) powered by large language models (LLMs). It argues that existing PIDSes suffer from a "knowledge silos" problem, hindering full automation and requiring significant manual intervention. To address this, the authors categorize PIDSes based on the types of knowledge they leverage: Attack Representation Knowledge (ARK), Threat Intelligence Knowledge (TIK), and Benign Behavior Knowledge (BBK).  They then introduce OMNISEC, a system integrating these knowledge types with an LLM to perform node-level anomaly detection and reconstruct attack graphs. The paper presents experimental results on public benchmark datasets, demonstrating OMNISEC's superior performance compared to state-of-the-art approaches. They also address limitations like LLM hallucination and discuss mitigation strategies.

**Critical Evaluation:**

The paper tackles a relevant problem: automating and improving the effectiveness of intrusion detection. The concept of "knowledge silos" is well-articulated and resonates with the challenges faced in practical security deployments. The categorization of knowledge types (ARK, TIK, BBK) provides a useful framework for analyzing existing PIDSes and designing new ones. Integrating these knowledge types with an LLM is a promising approach to overcome limitations of traditional PIDSes.

However, the paper also has weaknesses.

*   **Novelty:** While the combination of ARK, TIK, BBK and LLMs in PIDS is somewhat novel, individual components have been previously explored.  The novelty lies primarily in the *systematic integration* and application within the context of provenance-based intrusion detection. The "knowledge-driven" categorization, while useful, isn't a groundbreaking theoretical contribution. The use of RAG is a standard technique in LLM applications.
*   **Experimental Validation:** The experimental results showcase improvements compared to existing systems, however, there is a lack of explanation for the parameters or methods used by other systems which were re-implemented in the study. This makes the evaluation less fair and reliable for comparison. Additionally, while DARPA datasets are valuable, there needs to be more discussion for potential biases that are built into these datasets. The results could be vulnerable to data-leakage and over-fitting.
*   **Limitations:** The paper acknowledges limitations such as LLM hallucination and potential attack escape strategies.  However, more in-depth exploration is needed. For example, the risk of "retrieval poisoning" attacks and their potential mitigation deserves further consideration. The discussion around data privacy and compliance is relatively brief. Further analysis is required.
*   **Practical Considerations:**  The paper doesn't fully address the practical challenges of deploying OMNISEC in real-world environments, such as the cost of running LLMs, the maintenance of knowledge databases, and the scalability of the system for large networks.

**Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its potential to inspire further research on knowledge-driven PIDSes. The proposed framework and the OMNISEC system demonstrate the feasibility of using LLMs to enhance intrusion detection capabilities. It could stimulate the development of more sophisticated and automated threat detection systems.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper provides a clear and useful framework for understanding and improving PIDSes. It explores the use of LLMs in the area and shows significant improvements with the proposed method. However, the lack of more in-depth discussion with regard to experimental validation, potential biases, and limitations are critical problems that hold the paper back. Given the systematic approach to knowledge integration, promising results, and potential impact on the field, but taking into account the areas that need more attention, the paper warrants a rating of:

**Score: 6.5**

- **Classification**: cs.CR
- **Score**: 6/10

### WarmFed: Federated Learning with Warm-Start for Globalization and Personalization Via Personalized Diffusion Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03110v1)
- **Authors**: Tao Feng, Jie Zhang, Xiangjian Li, Rong Huang, Huashan Liu, Zhijie Wang
- **Abstract**: Federated Learning (FL) stands as a prominent distributed learning paradigm among multiple clients to achieve a unified global model without privacy leakage. In contrast to FL, Personalized federated learning aims at serving for each client in achieving persoanlized model. However, previous FL frameworks have grappled with a dilemma: the choice between developing a singular global model at the server to bolster globalization or nurturing personalized model at the client to accommodate personalization. Instead of making trade-offs, this paper commences its discourse from the pre-trained initialization, obtaining resilient global information and facilitating the development of both global and personalized models. Specifically, we propose a novel method called WarmFed to achieve this. WarmFed customizes Warm-start through personalized diffusion models, which are generated by local efficient fine-tunining (LoRA). Building upon the Warm-Start, we advance a server-side fine-tuning strategy to derive the global model, and propose a dynamic self-distillation (DSD) to procure more resilient personalized models simultaneously. Comprehensive experiments underscore the substantial gains of our approach across both global and personalized models, achieved within just one-shot and five communication(s).
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces WarmFed, a novel federated learning (FL) framework that aims to improve both globalization and personalization through a warm-start approach. Unlike traditional FL methods that start from random initialization, WarmFed leverages personalized diffusion models, fine-tuned locally with LoRA (Low-Rank Adaptation), to generate customized warm-starts for clients. The paper then proposes server-side fine-tuning strategies for globalization and dynamic self-distillation (DSD) for personalization. The results show that WarmFed achieves substantial gains in both global and personalized models with one-shot and five-shot communication settings, while maintaining privacy and low transmission costs.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The idea of using personalized diffusion models for warm-starting in FL is novel. It addresses the dilemma between globalization and personalization, which are often considered conflicting objectives. The combination of LoRA for efficient local fine-tuning and server-side DSD strategy further enhances the novelty. The method attempts to balance global knowledge acquisition and personalized adaptation in FL settings, and the specific implementation using diffusion models and knowledge distillation is unique within this context.

*   **Significance:** The paper's potential impact on the FL field is significant. By providing a customized initialization for each client, WarmFed could lead to faster convergence, better generalization, and improved personalization. The framework's privacy-preserving nature and low transmission costs make it practical for real-world applications with strict data confidentiality requirements. The improvements over existing methods, as demonstrated in the experiments, highlight the potential benefits of WarmFed.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Problem Formulation:** The paper clearly defines the problem of balancing globalization and personalization in FL and motivates the need for customized warm-start approaches.
    *   **Methodology:** WarmFed is well-designed and combines several techniques (diffusion models, LoRA, dynamic self-distillation) in a coherent framework.
    *   **Experimental Evaluation:** The paper presents comprehensive experiments on multiple datasets and compares WarmFed to several baselines, demonstrating its superior performance. The ablation studies provide insights into the contribution of individual components (fine-tuning, DSD).
    *   **Privacy Considerations:** The authors address the privacy concerns associated with transmitting client information and propose solutions to mitigate the risks.
    *   **Practical Considerations:** The paper discusses communication costs, computational complexity, and scalability, making it relevant for real-world deployment.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Diffusion Model Dependency:** The reliance on diffusion models for warm-start generation may limit the applicability of WarmFed to tasks where such models are readily available or easily trained.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:**  The performance of WarmFed may be sensitive to hyperparameters, such as the distillation temperature in DSD or the choice of LoRA parameters. The sensitivity to these factors could require careful tuning for each specific application.
    *   **Limited Theoretical Analysis:** Although the experimental results are compelling, the paper lacks theoretical analysis of the convergence properties and generalization bounds of WarmFed.
    *   **Scalability to Large Number of Clients**: Although, experiments show that with a large number of clients, the method is scalable, more investigation on the complexity in practical scenario is needed.

*   **Potential Influence:** This work could prompt further research into personalized initialization methods for FL, and the idea of leveraging generative models to create warm-starts could be adopted in other distributed learning settings. The proposed DSD strategy could also inspire new techniques for knowledge transfer and personalization in FL. The paper could influence future research in areas that emphasize the fusion of global and local information, while adhering to privacy constraints.

**Score: 7.5**

**Rationale:**

WarmFed presents a significant contribution to the field of federated learning, primarily through its innovative warm-start strategy and the effective integration of diffusion models and dynamic self-distillation. It addresses a relevant problem of trade-offs between global and personalized learning with strong empirical results, privacy considerations, and low transmission costs. The integration and customization of these different components within FL provides a novel framework. While the reliance on diffusion models might limit the breadth of applicability somewhat, the overall impact of these integrations demonstrates potential for advancement in the field. Therefore, a score of 7.5 acknowledges its strong, empirical contributions but also accounts for the need for more theoretical investigation and potential limitations with diffusion models.

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 7/10

### PromAssistant: Leveraging Large Language Models for Text-to-PromQL
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03114v1)
- **Authors**: Chenxi Zhang, Bicheng Zhang, Dingyu Yang, Xin Peng, Miao Chen, Senyu Xie, Gang Chen, Wei Bi, Wei Li
- **Abstract**: With the increasing complexity of modern online service systems, understanding the state and behavior of the systems is essential for ensuring their reliability and stability. Therefore, metric monitoring systems are widely used and become an important infrastructure in online service systems. Engineers usually interact with metrics data by manually writing domain-specific language (DSL) queries to achieve various analysis objectives. However, writing these queries can be challenging and time-consuming, as it requires engineers to have high programming skills and understand the context of the system. In this paper, we focus on PromQL, which is the metric query DSL provided by the widely used metric monitoring system Prometheus. We aim to simplify metrics querying by enabling engineers to interact with metrics data in Prometheus through natural language, and we call this task text-to-PromQL. Building upon the insight, this paper proposes PromAssistant, a Large Language Model-based text-to-PromQL framework. PromAssistant first uses a knowledge graph to describe the complex context of an online service system. Then, through the synergistic reasoning of LLMs and the knowledge graph, PromAssistant transforms engineers' natural language questions into PromQL queries. To evaluate PromAssistant, we manually construct the first text-to-PromQL benchmark dataset which contains 280 metric query questions. The experiment results show that PromAssistant is effective in text-to-PromQL and outperforms baseline approaches. To the best of our knowledge, this paper is the first study of text-to-PromQL, and PromAssistant pioneered the DSL generation framework for metric querying and analysis.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PromAssistant, an LLM-based framework designed to translate natural language queries into PromQL for Prometheus, a widely used metric monitoring system. The key innovation lies in leveraging a knowledge graph to represent the complex context of online service systems (metrics, components, and their relationships). PromAssistant uses this knowledge graph to enhance the reasoning capabilities of LLMs, enabling them to generate more accurate PromQL queries. The authors construct a novel text-to-PromQL benchmark dataset and evaluate PromAssistant against baseline approaches, demonstrating its effectiveness in generating correct PromQL. The ablation study reveals that metric knowledge and system component knowledge are crucial for achieving high performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper addresses a practical problem in online service system monitoring: the difficulty of writing PromQL queries. The core idea of combining a knowledge graph with LLMs for query generation is innovative in the specific context of PromQL.  While leveraging knowledge graphs with LLMs is not entirely new, its application to simplify metric querying and analysis in Prometheus is a valuable contribution. The construction of the first text-to-PromQL benchmark dataset is a significant addition to the field.  The authors also explicitly acknowledge the limitations of existing methods in code generation or SQL query translation highlighting the specific challenges of their new task.

*   **Significance:** The framework has the potential to significantly improve the productivity of engineers who work with Prometheus. By enabling natural language interaction, it lowers the barrier to entry for complex metrics querying and analysis. The construction of the text-to-PromQL dataset contributes to the advancement of research in this domain.  However, the evaluation is limited to a single, albeit large and complex, open-source microservice benchmark system. This raises questions about the generalizability of the results to other system architectures and monitoring environments. Furthermore, the baseline prompts and the few-shot training are not rigorously compared against modern sophisticated prompt engineering strategies, thereby affecting the comparative analysis.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Addresses a practical and relevant problem.
    *   Proposes a novel framework that combines knowledge graphs and LLMs.
    *   Constructs a new benchmark dataset for text-to-PromQL.
    *   Evaluates PromAssistant against multiple baselines with a thorough ablation study, although the prompt strategies are comparatively basic.
    *   Clear explanation of methodology and results.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Evaluation limited to the TrainTicket benchmark. The generalizability to more diverse production environments is not fully established.
    *   Comparison to more sophisticated few-shot learning techniques or advanced prompt engineering would strengthen the evaluation.
    *   The knowledge graph construction may be limited to the system setup, making the method itself not easily generalizable across domains.

*   **Potential Impact:** The research can influence the development of more user-friendly metric monitoring systems and the adoption of knowledge graph-enhanced LLMs in AIOps and related domains. The dataset can serve as a foundation for further research.

**Rationale for the Score:**

The paper presents a solid and well-executed approach to a practical problem, making it a valuable addition to the field. It's particularly strong in its clear methodology, the creation of a new benchmark, and the demonstrated improvements over basic baselines. While the novelty of the KG-enhanced LLM is incremental, its application in simplifying PromQL query generation is important and potentially impactful. However, its relative simplicity makes it less impactful than transformative work that presents new frameworks.
