### JamMa: Ultra-lightweight Local Feature Matching with Joint Mamba
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03437v1)
- **Authors**: Xiaoyong Lu, Songlin Du
- **Abstract**: Existing state-of-the-art feature matchers capture long-range dependencies with Transformers but are hindered by high spatial complexity, leading to demanding training and highlatency inference. Striking a better balance between performance and efficiency remains a challenge in feature matching. Inspired by the linear complexity O(N) of Mamba, we propose an ultra-lightweight Mamba-based matcher, named JamMa, which converges on a single GPU and achieves an impressive performance-efficiency balance in inference. To unlock the potential of Mamba for feature matching, we propose Joint Mamba with a scan-merge strategy named JEGO, which enables: (1) Joint scan of two images to achieve high-frequency mutual interaction, (2) Efficient scan with skip steps to reduce sequence length, (3) Global receptive field, and (4) Omnidirectional feature representation. With the above properties, the JEGO strategy significantly outperforms the scan-merge strategies proposed in VMamba and EVMamba in the feature matching task. Compared to attention-based sparse and semi-dense matchers, JamMa demonstrates a superior balance between performance and efficiency, delivering better performance with less than 50% of the parameters and FLOPs.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "JamMa: Ultra-lightweight Local Feature Matching with Joint Mamba":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces JamMa, a novel ultra-lightweight local feature matcher leveraging the Mamba architecture. JamMa addresses the limitations of existing Transformer-based matchers by employing a Joint Mamba network combined with a novel scan-merge strategy called JEGO. JEGO facilitates joint scanning of image pairs, efficient processing with skip steps, a global receptive field, and omnidirectional feature representation. The authors claim JamMa achieves a superior performance-efficiency balance compared to attention-based sparse and semi-dense matchers, using significantly fewer parameters and FLOPs while maintaining competitive accuracy.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Efficiency:** The primary strength is the demonstrated efficiency. The paper convincingly shows JamMa's lower computational cost (parameters, FLOPs, and runtime) compared to many existing methods. This is a significant advantage for real-time applications and resource-constrained environments.
    *   **Novelty in Mamba Application:** Applying Mamba to the feature matching task is a novel contribution.  The design of the JEGO strategy to adapt Mamba's sequence processing to the 2D image matching problem is a key innovation. The idea of joint scanning is relatively new and has the potential to be explored further.
    *   **Good Experimental Results:** The paper provides substantial experimental evidence, including comparisons on MegaDepth and HPatches datasets. The results suggest that JamMa achieves a good performance-efficiency trade-off, particularly when compared to other lightweight methods.
    *   **Clear Ablation Studies:** The ablation studies are well-designed and provide insights into the importance of different components of the JEGO strategy (joint scan, aggregator, etc.). This strengthens the claims about the effectiveness of the proposed design choices.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Limited Generalizability:** Although the authors tested on ScanNet dataset, the paper lacks rigorous justification for the decreased generalizability in indoor scenes. More detailed and diverse testing, especially zero-shot, is important. This issue could become crucial when tested with real-world scenarios.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The core idea of leveraging Mamba is interesting but the actual performance improvement relative to other lightweight methods, while present, might be considered incremental rather than revolutionary. JamMa beats transformer-based methods because of its efficient mamba architecture, and is only slightly better than other mamba architectures in this area.
    *   **Complexity of Implementation:** While the paper describes the JEGO strategy, the interplay of different components (joint scan, four-directional scan, aggregator) might be complex to implement and optimize in practice.  More details on implementation challenges would be valuable.

*   **Significance and Potential Influence:**
    *   The paper's contribution lies in demonstrating the feasibility of using Mamba for feature matching and in proposing a practical and efficient architecture. This could encourage further research into applying Mamba to other computer vision tasks.
    *   The emphasis on efficiency is particularly relevant given the growing demand for real-time and low-power computer vision systems. JamMa could be a valuable building block for such systems.
    *   The work provides insights into the design of efficient scan-merge strategies for visual Mamba models, which could be useful for researchers working on other visual Mamba-based architectures.

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the balance of strengths and weaknesses, I assign a score of 7.
* The paper presents a novel architecture (JamMa) with clear advantages in efficiency over existing feature matching algorithms. The design is motivated, and the performance is validated by multiple experiments. However, the limitations of this method include limited generalizability and implementational challenges. Overall, the method achieves a great efficiency with decent performance, but a bigger test set to prove generalizability is warranted.

- **Score**: 7/10

### Taxation Perspectives from Large Language Models: A Case Study on Additional Tax Penalties
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03444v1)
- **Authors**: Eunkyung Choi, Young Jin Suh, Hun Park, Wonseok Hwang
- **Abstract**: How capable are large language models (LLMs) in the domain of taxation? Although numerous studies have explored the legal domain in general, research dedicated to taxation remain scarce. Moreover, the datasets used in these studies are either simplified, failing to reflect the real-world complexities, or unavailable as open source. To address this gap, we introduce PLAT, a new benchmark designed to assess the ability of LLMs to predict the legitimacy of additional tax penalties. PLAT is constructed to evaluate LLMs' understanding of tax law, particularly in cases where resolving the issue requires more than just applying related statutes. Our experiments with six LLMs reveal that their baseline capabilities are limited, especially when dealing with conflicting issues that demand a comprehensive understanding. However, we found that enabling retrieval, self-reasoning, and discussion among multiple agents with specific role assignments, this limitation can be mitigated.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces PLAT, a new benchmark dataset designed to evaluate the capabilities of large language models (LLMs) in understanding Korean tax law, specifically in determining the legitimacy of additional tax penalties. The dataset focuses on cases where resolving the issue requires more than simply applying related statutes, demanding a deeper understanding of legal principles and contextual factors. The authors evaluate six LLMs (two open-source and four commercial) and find that their baseline performance is limited, particularly when dealing with complex and conflicting issues. They then explore how integrating agent functionality, such as retrieval augmentation, self-reasoning, and multi-agent collaboration, can mitigate these limitations and improve performance.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Novel Dataset:** The primary strength of the paper is the introduction of PLAT.  The focus on cases that require more than simple statutory look-up is a significant step forward. The dataset fills a gap in existing legal NLP benchmarks, most of which are either overly simplified or lack open-source availability.  The manual construction of the dataset by tax professionals lends credibility to its quality and relevance.  Furthermore, the attention to details, the manual reviews conducted by experts and efforts to remove biases in the extraction process from the precedents strengthens the value of the dataset.
    *   **Focus on a Specific Legal Domain:**  By concentrating on taxation, the paper addresses a relatively under-explored area within legal NLP. This specialization allows for a deeper dive into the intricacies of tax law and its nuances.
    *   **Agent-Based Exploration:** The exploration of agent-based techniques (RAG, self-reasoning, multi-agent systems) is valuable. This reveals potential avenues for improving LLM performance in complex legal tasks.  The study's observation that simply combining all functionalities doesn't necessarily yield the best results is also a significant finding.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Limited Scope of PLAT:** While PLAT is a valuable contribution, its focus on a specific aspect of Korean tax law (additional penalties) limits its generalizability. The dataset's size (50 examples) is also relatively small, although this is understandable given the manual creation process.
    *   **Dependency on Korean Legal Precedents:** The use of Korean precedents introduces a language and legal system dependency that may make it difficult for researchers unfamiliar with the Korean legal context to fully utilize the dataset. While an English translation will be made available, the subtleties may be lost in translation.
    *   **Incremental Improvement:** The reported improvements through agent-based approaches, while positive, are not dramatically high. The maximum +11% F1 gain is valuable but may not be transformative.
    *   **Lack of In-depth Error Analysis:** While the paper mentions some error patterns, a more detailed qualitative error analysis could provide deeper insights into the specific challenges LLMs face in understanding tax law. For example, a more detailed characterization of the “hard” cases would have been beneficial.
    *   **Comparison with existing LLM-based Agent legal research tool** A comparison with similar LLM-based agent solutions in other legal domains (for example, Lex Machina, CARA) or even general legal question answering system (such as WestLaw AI) would contextualize PLAT's contributions relative to industry-leading approaches.
*   **Novelty:**

    *   The novelty lies primarily in the specialized PLAT dataset targeting a previously under-explored aspect of tax law, forcing models to go beyond simple statutory lookup.

*   **Significance:**

    *   The significance stems from the challenge that it raises to other legal NLP researchers. The use of taxation introduces new considerations such as the need to follow legal principle and also contextual factors. This will likely motivate researchers to investigate more complex methods.

**Justification:**

The paper makes a solid contribution by introducing a new, specialized dataset and exploring techniques for enhancing LLM performance in a complex legal domain. However, the limited scope of the dataset, its dependency on Korean legal precedents, and the incremental nature of the improvements through agent-based approaches temper its overall impact. The PLAT dataset will be a useful tool for researchers in the field, but its specific focus means it may not have a broad influence on general legal NLP. A comprehensive and complete error analysis will improve its value. Despite these limitations, the dataset will make significant contribution in assessing LLM's understanding of tax law with complex scenarios.

### Unified Mind Model: Reimagining Autonomous Agents in the LLM Era
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03459v1)
- **Authors**: Pengbo Hu, Xiang Ying
- **Abstract**: Large language models (LLMs) have recently demonstrated remarkable capabilities across domains, tasks, and languages (e.g., ChatGPT and GPT-4), reviving the research of general autonomous agents with human-like cognitive abilities.Such human-level agents require semantic comprehension and instruction-following capabilities, which exactly fall into the strengths of LLMs.Although there have been several initial attempts to build human-level agents based on LLMs, the theoretical foundation remains a challenging open problem. In this paper, we propose a novel theoretical cognitive architecture, the Unified Mind Model (UMM), which offers guidance to facilitate the rapid creation of autonomous agents with human-level cognitive abilities. Specifically, our UMM starts with the global workspace theory and further leverage LLMs to enable the agent with various cognitive abilities, such as multi-modal perception, planning, reasoning, tool use, learning, memory, reflection and motivation. Building upon UMM, we then develop an agent-building engine, MindOS, which allows users to quickly create domain-/task-specific autonomous agents without any programming effort.
- **Summary**: Okay, I will provide a summary and a critical evaluation with a novelty/significance score for this paper.

**Summary:**

The paper "Unified Mind Model: Reimagining Autonomous Agents in the LLM Era" proposes a new cognitive architecture, the Unified Mind Model (UMM), for building autonomous agents using Large Language Models (LLMs).  The UMM is inspired by the Global Workspace Theory (GWT) and integrates LLMs to emulate human-level cognitive abilities such as perception, planning, reasoning, tool use, learning, memory, reflection, and motivation.  The authors also present MindOS, an agent-building engine based on UMM, designed to enable users to quickly create domain-specific autonomous agents without requiring programming expertise. MindOS features a four-tier hierarchical structure, consisting of Foundation Model Module, Specialist Module, Central Processing Module and Driver System. The paper details the architecture, components, and learning mechanisms of both UMM and MindOS, offering insights into their potential for advancing the development of intelligent agents.

**Critical Evaluation:**

This paper tackles an interesting and timely problem: how to effectively leverage the capabilities of LLMs to create more sophisticated and human-like autonomous agents.  The idea of grounding an agent architecture in a well-established cognitive theory like GWT is promising.  However, several aspects warrant critical consideration.

*   **Novelty:** While the *combination* of GWT with LLMs is a good idea, it's not entirely revolutionary. The idea of using LLMs as a central "controller" or "planner" in agent architectures has already been explored in various forms (e.g., AutoGPT, BabyAGI, and related works mentioned in the paper). What sets it apart is the use of a rigorous cognitive framework. The novelty lies in the specific instantiation of GWT with LLMs and the development of MindOS to make this more accessible. Whether this instantiation offers substantial improvements over existing approaches is not clear from the paper.

*   **Significance:** If the UMM and MindOS can deliver on their promise, the paper could significantly impact the field of autonomous agents. A key factor will be demonstrating the effectiveness of the proposed architecture in real-world applications. The paper lacks empirical validation. There are no experiments or performance comparisons against existing agent architectures. Without such evidence, it's difficult to assess the actual advantages of UMM/MindOS. The theoretical architecture is sound, but the real significance hinges on its practical performance. In addition, its reliance on LLMs means it is affected by the limitations inherent to LLMs, especially regarding factuality, reasoning, and cost.

*   **Strengths:** The paper is well-structured and clearly explains the theoretical underpinnings of UMM. The connection to GWT provides a solid foundation and differentiates it from purely engineering-driven agent designs. The MindOS concept is also appealing, as it aims to simplify the development of autonomous agents, potentially lowering the barrier to entry for researchers and practitioners. The modular design seems to be a strength, offering flexibility in adding functionality and skills.

*   **Weaknesses:** The biggest weakness is the lack of empirical validation. The paper describes the architecture but doesn't provide concrete evidence that it works effectively. Performance metrics, comparative studies, or even detailed case studies are missing. The claims regarding improved performance and efficiency remain speculative without such evidence. Moreover, the paper states that it was completed in March 2023 but only released now due to "commercial issues." This raises concerns about its current relevance, given the rapid progress in the field of LLMs and agent architectures. The "commercial issues" also remain unclear.

*   **Potential Influence:** The UMM concept and MindOS platform *could* influence future agent designs by promoting a more theoretically grounded approach. The emphasis on modularity and ease of use could also encourage broader adoption of LLM-based agents. However, its influence depends heavily on demonstrating its effectiveness and addressing the limitations discussed above. If further work demonstrates a clear advantage over existing approaches, the paper could gain more traction.

**Overall:**

The paper presents a promising architecture for building autonomous agents, leveraging the strengths of LLMs and a cognitive theory. However, the lack of empirical validation and the delayed release limit its current significance. More work is needed to demonstrate the real-world performance and advantages of UMM/MindOS before its full potential can be realized.

**Score: 5.5**

**Rationale for the score:**

I am assigning a score of 5.5 because while the paper provides a theoretically interesting architecture and a potentially useful platform (MindOS), it lacks crucial empirical evidence. The conceptual framework is moderately novel in its specific combination of elements, but similar ideas have been explored.  Without experimental results, it is impossible to determine its practical value compared to other approaches. The "commercial issues" and delayed release further detract from the paper's immediate impact. The score reflects that it's a solid conceptual contribution but requires significant further work to demonstrate its practical significance.

- **Classification**: cs.AI
- **Score**: 5/10

### Visualising Policy-Reward Interplay to Inform Zeroth-Order Preference Optimisation of Large Language Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03460v1)
- **Authors**: Alessio Galatolo, Zhenbang Dai, Katie Winkle, Meriem Beloucif
- **Abstract**: Fine-tuning LLMs with first-order methods like back-propagation is computationally intensive. Zeroth-Order (ZO) optimisation, using function evaluations instead of gradients, reduces memory usage but suffers from slow convergence in high-dimensional models. As a result, ZO research in LLMs has mostly focused on classification, overlooking more complex generative tasks. In this paper, we introduce ZOPrO, a novel ZO algorithm designed for \textit{Preference Optimisation} in LLMs. We begin by analysing the interplay between policy and reward models during traditional (first-order) Preference Optimisation, uncovering patterns in their relative updates. Guided by these insights, we adapt Simultaneous Perturbation Stochastic Approximation (SPSA) with a targeted sampling strategy to accelerate convergence. Through experiments on summarisation, machine translation, and conversational assistants, we demonstrate that our method consistently enhances reward signals while achieving convergence times comparable to first-order methods. While it falls short of some state-of-the-art methods, our work is the first to apply Zeroth-Order methods to Preference Optimisation in LLMs, going beyond classification tasks and paving the way for a largely unexplored research direction. Code and visualisations are available at https://github.com/alessioGalatolo/VisZOPrO
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces ZOPrO, a novel zeroth-order optimization algorithm designed for preference optimization in large language models (LLMs).  It addresses the computational expense of fine-tuning LLMs with first-order methods by leveraging zeroth-order optimization, which reduces memory usage but typically suffers from slow convergence.  The core idea is to analyze the interplay between policy and reward models during traditional preference optimization, using visualization techniques to understand their relative updates. This analysis reveals patterns, particularly the orthogonality of reward model updates to policy updates and the influence of previous updates on upcoming ones. Guided by these insights, the authors adapt Simultaneous Perturbation Stochastic Approximation (SPSA) with a targeted sampling strategy to accelerate convergence. The method is evaluated on summarization, machine translation, and conversational assistant tasks. The results demonstrate enhanced reward signals and convergence times comparable to first-order methods.  Code and visualizations are provided.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's main contribution lies in applying zeroth-order optimization to the preference optimization task in LLMs, especially for complex generative tasks (summarization, translation, conversational assistants) which differentiates it from much prior ZO work that focuses on classification. The visualization and analysis of the policy-reward interaction is also a novel aspect. The targeted SPSA sampling strategy, informed by these observations, further adds to the novelty. Prior guided strategy relies on oracles while this work uses previous updates which is a significant enhancement.

*   **Significance:** The significance stems from the potential to reduce the computational cost and memory requirements of fine-tuning LLMs, making it more accessible, especially for on-device personalization and larger models. The focus on preference optimization is also significant, given the increasing importance of aligning LLMs with human preferences. While the performance might not surpass state-of-the-art methods, it opens up a largely unexplored research direction.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Addressing a Relevant Problem:** Fine-tuning LLMs is computationally demanding, making the exploration of memory-efficient alternatives like zeroth-order optimization highly relevant. Preference optimization is also a key area for LLM development.
    *   **Empirical Analysis and Insights:** The visualization and analysis of policy-reward dynamics offer valuable insights into the training process, going beyond black-box optimization. This can contribute to a better understanding of LLM training dynamics in general.
    *   **Well-Defined Method:** ZOPrO is clearly defined and presented, including the adaptive perturbation strategy and convergence analysis, adding to its value.
    *   **Experimental Validation:** Demonstrating the effectiveness of ZOPrO on multiple tasks (summarization, translation, and dialogue) strengthens the claims and shows the generalizability of the approach.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Performance Compared to SOTA:** While the method shows promise, it falls short of achieving state-of-the-art results. The improvements brought are rather small.
    *   **Limited Model Scale:** Experiments are conducted on a relatively small model (LLaMA-3.2 1B). Scalability to larger models needs to be demonstrated.
    *   **Hyperparameter Sensitivity:** Similar to other ZO methods, ZOPrO is sensitive to hyperparameter tuning, which can be a practical challenge.
    *   **Evaluation Metric:** The paper relies on reward improvement as the primary evaluation metric, which isn't as reliable to evaluate output quality compared to other metrics. Direct comparison of model outputs across different methods using automated or human ranking would give a clearer picture of its performance.

*   **Potential Impact:** Despite the limitations, the paper is impactful in the sense that it:

    *   Opens a new avenue of research for applying zeroth-order methods to preference optimization in LLMs, going beyond classification.
    *   Provides a valuable framework for visualizing and understanding policy-reward interactions.
    *   Presents a practical and effective zeroth-order optimization algorithm with potential for memory savings.

**Overall Score and Justification:**

I assign a score of **7**.

*Rationale:** The paper demonstrates good novelty by introducing zeroth-order optimization to a fairly untouched area, preference optimization of generative tasks in LLMs. It uses visualization to gain insights, implements a reasonable algorithm, and performs multi-task experiments. While not achieving state-of-the-art performance, it offers computational advantages and a valuable framework for future research. The weaknesses regarding model scale, hyperparameter sensitivity, and evaluation metrics hold it back from a higher score. ZOPrO does not significantly outperform RLOO or DPO so far, and it's rather more of a suggestion towards an interesting avenue rather than a concrete solution. But, the core idea of incorporating learned information to guide the random direction in SPSA is promising. A score higher than 7 would suggest this work should already be regarded as high-impact, which does not seem to be necessarily the case.

Score: 7

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Open-Source Large Language Models as Multilingual Crowdworkers: Synthesizing Open-Domain Dialogues in Several Languages With No Examples in Targets and No Machine Translation
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03462v1)
- **Authors**: Ahmed Njifenjou, Virgile Sucal, Bassam Jabaian, Fabrice Lefèvre
- **Abstract**: The prevailing paradigm in the domain of Open-Domain Dialogue agents predominantly focuses on the English language, encompassing both models and datasets. Furthermore, the financial and temporal investments required for crowdsourcing such datasets for finetuning are substantial, particularly when multiple languages are involved. Fortunately, advancements in Large Language Models (LLMs) have unveiled a plethora of possibilities across diverse tasks. Specifically, instruction-tuning has enabled LLMs to execute tasks based on natural language instructions, occasionally surpassing the performance of human crowdworkers. Additionally, these models possess the capability to function in various languages within a single thread. Consequently, to generate new samples in different languages, we propose leveraging these capabilities to replicate the data collection process. We introduce a pipeline for generating Open-Domain Dialogue data in multiple Target Languages using LLMs, with demonstrations provided in a unique Source Language. By eschewing explicit Machine Translation in this approach, we enhance the adherence to language-specific nuances. We apply this methodology to the PersonaChat dataset. To enhance the openness of generated dialogues and mimic real life scenarii, we added the notion of speech events corresponding to the type of conversation the speakers are involved in and also that of common ground which represents the premises of a conversation.
- **Summary**: Here is a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper tackles the challenge of creating open-domain dialogue datasets in multiple languages, especially when resources are scarce. The authors propose a novel pipeline that leverages the multilingual abilities of large language models (LLMs) to generate new dialogue samples in target languages. The key innovation is the use of instruction-tuning to guide LLMs, along with techniques to enhance language-specific nuances and address the open domain paradox (ODP). This is achieved without relying on machine translation (MT) from a source language, thereby avoiding the quality issues associated with it. The pipeline generates persona-based dialogue data, incorporating common ground and speech events, to enhance realism and conversational diversity. The method is applied to the PersonaChat dataset to generate a new multilingual dataset called MOUD, encompassing 29 languages. The paper then presents both automatic and human evaluations of the generated data, along with baseline fine-tuning experiments.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a valuable and relatively novel approach to multilingual dialogue data generation. While LLMs have been used for data generation in NLP tasks, their systematic application to create high-quality, multilingual open-domain dialogue datasets *without* MT is a significant contribution. The inclusion of common ground and diverse speech event types goes beyond previous efforts.
*   **Significance:** Open-domain dialogue datasets are critical for training and evaluating conversational AI systems. The scarcity of such datasets in languages other than English is a major bottleneck. MOUD represents a step toward a more inclusive and globally relevant research landscape in conversational AI. It also offers a way to collect dataset without the risk of crowdworkers taking advantage of LLMs in disguise to contribute for data creation.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **MT-free approach:** Avoiding MT enhances language-specific characteristics in the generated dialogues.
    *   **ODP Mitigation:** Incorporation of common ground and diverse speech events addresses the limitations of traditional open-domain dialogue datasets.
    *   **Multilingual Focus:** The generation of a dataset in 29 languages significantly expands the availability of resources for multilingual conversational AI.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:**  The use of automatic metrics (BERTScore), syntax analysis, LLM-as-a-judge evaluations, and human evaluations provides a fairly comprehensive assessment of data quality.
    *   **Reproducibility:** By using open-source LLMs, the authors aim to increase reproducibility, a valuable practice given the frequent reliance on closed-source models.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **LLM Reliance:** The quality of the generated data is highly dependent on the capabilities of the LLMs used. If LLMs have biases or lack the depth of understanding in certain languages, this will be reflected in the data.
    *   **Evaluation Limitations:** The reliance on LLM-as-a-judge evaluations can be questioned because of the inherent problems with LLMs and their limitations which are outlined by the authors in the limitations section. While the authors addressed this with human evaluations, obtaining sufficient human judgments for all languages is challenging, limiting the scope of comprehensive quality control.
    *   **Baseline Performance:** The experiments done as part of the baseline were with relatively smaller LLM models and may not fully show the power of what has been generated by using instruction-tuned LLMs.
    *   **Lack of quantitative comparison with MT approaches:** It would be beneficial to show some numerical comparison in terms of language metrics etc. to show what their approach is truly superior.
*   **Potential Influence:** The work can influence the field by:

    *   Encouraging the creation of more multilingual conversational AI systems.
    *   Providing a valuable resource for researchers studying cross-lingual dialogue.
    *   Inspiring new approaches to data generation that minimize reliance on expensive human annotation.
    *   Raising awareness of the open-domain paradox and promoting the development of more realistic dialogue datasets.

Score: 7.5

**Rationale:** The paper provides a significant contribution to the field by demonstrating a practical and effective approach to multilingual dialogue data generation that addresses several key challenges.  It shows a practical solution by creating the MOUD dataset that also addressed some pitfalls that are otherwise apparent for PersonaChat Dataset. However, the reliance on LLMs introduces some limitations, and the evaluation has room for further improvement. The novelty and potential impact of the work justify a high score, though some weaknesses prevent it from being exceptional.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### Generative Artificial Intelligence in Robotic Manipulation: A Survey
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03464v1)
- **Authors**: Kun Zhang, Peng Yun, Jun Cen, Junhao Cai, Didi Zhu, Hangjie Yuan, Chao Zhao, Tao Feng, Michael Yu Wang, Qifeng Chen, Jia Pan, Bo Yang, Hua Chen
- **Abstract**: This survey provides a comprehensive review on recent advancements of generative learning models in robotic manipulation, addressing key challenges in the field. Robotic manipulation faces critical bottlenecks, including significant challenges in insufficient data and inefficient data acquisition, long-horizon and complex task planning, and the multi-modality reasoning ability for robust policy learning performance across diverse environments. To tackle these challenges, this survey introduces several generative model paradigms, including Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs), Variational Autoencoders (VAEs), diffusion models, probabilistic flow models, and autoregressive models, highlighting their strengths and limitations. The applications of these models are categorized into three hierarchical layers: the Foundation Layer, focusing on data generation and reward generation; the Intermediate Layer, covering language, code, visual, and state generation; and the Policy Layer, emphasizing grasp generation and trajectory generation. Each layer is explored in detail, along with notable works that have advanced the state of the art. Finally, the survey outlines future research directions and challenges, emphasizing the need for improved efficiency in data utilization, better handling of long-horizon tasks, and enhanced generalization across diverse robotic scenarios. All the related resources, including research papers, open-source data, and projects, are collected for the community in https://github.com/GAI4Manipulation/AwesomeGAIManipulation
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the survey paper on Generative Artificial Intelligence in Robotic Manipulation:

**Summary:**

The survey paper comprehensively reviews recent advancements in applying generative AI models to robotic manipulation. It identifies key challenges in the field, including data scarcity, long-horizon task planning, and the need for multi-modal reasoning. The authors categorize generative models (GANs, VAEs, diffusion models, probabilistic flow models, and autoregressive models) and their applications across three hierarchical layers: a Foundation Layer (data/reward generation), an Intermediate Layer (language, code, visual, state generation), and a Policy Layer (grasp/trajectory generation). The survey discusses notable works in each area and outlines future research directions, emphasizing improved data utilization efficiency, handling of long-horizon tasks, and enhanced generalization.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's novelty stems from its specific focus on generative AI's role in addressing core challenges in robotic manipulation. While other surveys have addressed robotics or foundation models broadly, this work provides a targeted perspective that bridges these areas. The hierarchical categorization is a useful framework for understanding how different generative models contribute at various levels of the manipulation pipeline.
*   **Significance:** Robotic manipulation is a crucial area for achieving advanced autonomy. Generative AI offers promising solutions to traditionally difficult problems. Therefore, the survey is significant as it highlights the transformative potential of these models in enhancing robotic capabilities. By organizing and synthesizing a wide range of research, it can serve as a valuable resource for researchers and practitioners in the field.
*   **Strengths:**

    *   **Comprehensive Scope:** The survey covers a wide range of generative models and their applications, providing a broad overview of the field.
    *   **Clear Structure:** The hierarchical organization (Foundation, Intermediate, Policy layers) allows for easy understanding and navigation of the material.
    *   **Detailed Discussion:** The paper provides detailed discussions of individual works, highlighting their contributions and limitations.
    *   **Future Directions:** The survey identifies key areas for future research, guiding further investigation in the field.
*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   **Depth vs. Breadth:** While comprehensive, the paper could benefit from a more in-depth analysis of specific generative model architectures and their underlying mechanisms.
    *   **Critical Evaluation:** The survey focuses primarily on summarizing existing works, and could delve deeper into comparing these works and evaluating their trade-offs.
    *   **Real-world Validation:** Although many techniques are surveyed, the performance limitations, especially in complex real-world settings, should be elaborated upon.
*   **Potential Influence:** The survey's potential influence lies in its ability to guide future research efforts. By identifying promising areas for development and highlighting current limitations, it can help accelerate progress in applying generative AI to robotic manipulation. It can also serve as a valuable educational resource for newcomers to the field.

**Justification of Score:**

Given the paper's novelty, significance, comprehensive nature, and well-defined structure, it constitutes a valuable contribution to the robotic manipulation and generative AI communities. While it has minor weaknesses related to depth of analysis and critical evaluation, its strengths outweigh these limitations. The identified future directions section also contributes to its impact.

Score: 7.5

- **Classification**: cs.RO
- **Score**: 7/10

### DTU-Net: A Multi-Scale Dilated Transformer Network for Nonlinear Hyperspectral Unmixing
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03465v1)
- **Authors**: ChenTong Wang, Jincheng Gao, Fei Zhu, Abderrahim Halimi, C'edric Richard
- **Abstract**: Transformers have shown significant success in hyperspectral unmixing (HU). However, challenges remain. While multi-scale and long-range spatial correlations are essential in unmixing tasks, current Transformer-based unmixing networks, built on Vision Transformer (ViT) or Swin-Transformer, struggle to capture them effectively. Additionally, current Transformer-based unmixing networks rely on the linear mixing model, which lacks the flexibility to accommodate scenarios where nonlinear effects are significant. To address these limitations, we propose a multi-scale Dilated Transformer-based unmixing network for nonlinear HU (DTU-Net). The encoder employs two branches. The first one performs multi-scale spatial feature extraction using Multi-Scale Dilated Attention (MSDA) in the Dilated Transformer, which varies dilation rates across attention heads to capture long-range and multi-scale spatial correlations. The second one performs spectral feature extraction utilizing 3D-CNNs with channel attention. The outputs from both branches are then fused to integrate multi-scale spatial and spectral information, which is subsequently transformed to estimate the abundances. The decoder is designed to accommodate both linear and nonlinear mixing scenarios. Its interpretability is enhanced by explicitly modeling the relationships between endmembers, abundances, and nonlinear coefficients in accordance with the polynomial post-nonlinear mixing model (PPNMM). Experiments on synthetic and real datasets validate the effectiveness of the proposed DTU-Net compared to PPNMM-derived methods and several advanced unmixing networks.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces DTU-Net, a novel multi-scale Dilated Transformer-based unmixing network for nonlinear hyperspectral unmixing (HU).  DTU-Net addresses limitations of previous Transformer-based HU methods, which struggle with capturing multi-scale and long-range spatial correlations effectively and often rely on the linear mixing model (LMM).  The encoder of DTU-Net uses two branches: one employing Multi-Scale Dilated Attention (MSDA) in a Dilated Transformer to capture spatial correlations at varying scales, and the other utilizing 3D-CNNs with channel attention for spectral feature extraction. The features from both branches are fused.  The decoder is based on the polynomial post-nonlinear mixing model (PPNMM), explicitly modeling relationships between endmembers, abundances, and nonlinear coefficients. Experiments on synthetic and real datasets demonstrate the effectiveness of DTU-Net compared to existing methods. The work shows an improved method over the traditional BMM-based methods by pixel-wise feature extraction.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper exhibits moderate novelty. The use of a Dilated Transformer with MSDA for hyperspectral unmixing is a significant contribution, particularly in capturing multi-scale spatial dependencies in a more efficient way compared to standard Vision Transformers or Swin Transformers. The integration of 3D-CNNs for spectral feature extraction in conjunction with the Dilated Transformer branch is also a novel design element. The explicit modeling of nonlinear coefficients based on the PPNMM in the decoder is a differentiating factor compared to methods that implicitly model nonlinearities or treat coefficients as network parameters. The learning coefficients as pixel-wise features rather than network parameters is also an additional novelty.

*   **Significance:** The paper's significance lies in its ability to address key limitations of existing hyperspectral unmixing methods.  By effectively capturing both multi-scale spatial dependencies and spectral information, DTU-Net demonstrates superior performance on complex scenes with varying target scales. The decoder's explicit modeling of nonlinear mixing scenarios enhances interpretability, and the pixel-wise estimation of nonlinear coefficients improves adaptability across datasets. Further, the experimental results validate the effectiveness of DTU-Net in both endmember extraction and abundance estimation across various synthetic and real datasets. There is some concern over the comparison only with PPNMM-derived and AE/CNN/Transformer methods, it leaves a gap for a clear comparison against other mixing models, such as GBM.

*   **Strengths:**

    *   The Dilated Transformer design allows effective capture of multi-scale spatial information with reduced computational cost compared to ViT.
    *   The fusion of spatial and spectral features enhances unmixing performance.
    *   The explicit PPNMM-based decoder improves interpretability and adaptability.
    *   Comprehensive experiments on diverse datasets provide strong evidence of DTU-Net's effectiveness.

*   **Weaknesses:**

    *   The paper could benefit from more in-depth analysis of the computational complexity of DTU-Net compared to other methods.
    *   There is limited discussion of the limitations of the PPNMM model itself and potential scenarios where it may not be appropriate.
    *   There is lack of discussion of computational complexity of the method compared to state-of-the-art unmixing algorithms.

*   **Potential Influence:** DTU-Net has the potential to influence future research in hyperspectral unmixing by providing a strong baseline for further development of Transformer-based methods and encouraging more explicit modeling of nonlinear mixing scenarios. The use of dilated convolutions and attention mechanisms could inspire new architectures for capturing spatial dependencies in hyperspectral images.

**Score: 7**

**Rationale:** The paper presents a novel and well-designed network for hyperspectral unmixing. While not groundbreaking in every aspect, the specific combination of a Dilated Transformer encoder with a PPNMM-based decoder and pixel-wise estimation of nonlinear coefficients provides a significant improvement over existing approaches. The comprehensive experimental results validate the effectiveness of the proposed method. There is room for improvement in areas such as computational complexity analysis and discussion of the limitations, preventing a higher score. This is a relevant and well-executed work that makes a valuable contribution to the field. The pixel-wise extraction is the main novelty.

- **Classification**: cs.CV
- **Score**: 7/10

### State-offset Tuning: State-based Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning for State Space Models
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03499v1)
- **Authors**: Wonjun Kang, Kevin Galim, Yuchen Zeng, Minjae Lee, Hyung Il Koo, Nam Ik Cho
- **Abstract**: State Space Models (SSMs) have emerged as efficient alternatives to Transformers, mitigating their quadratic computational cost. However, the application of Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) methods to SSMs remains largely unexplored. In particular, prompt-based methods like Prompt Tuning and Prefix-Tuning, which are widely used in Transformers, do not perform well on SSMs. To address this, we propose state-based methods as a superior alternative to prompt-based methods. This new family of methods naturally stems from the architectural characteristics of SSMs. State-based methods adjust state-related features directly instead of depending on external prompts. Furthermore, we introduce a novel state-based PEFT method: State-offset Tuning. At every timestep, our method directly affects the state at the current step, leading to more effective adaptation. Through extensive experiments across diverse datasets, we demonstrate the effectiveness of our method. Code is available at https://github.com/furiosa-ai/ssm-state-tuning.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "State-offset Tuning: State-based Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning for State Space Models":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces state-based Parameter-Efficient Fine-Tuning (PEFT) methods for State Space Models (SSMs). The authors argue that existing prompt-based PEFT methods, effective for Transformers, are less suitable for SSMs. They propose a new family of methods that directly modify the intrinsic state-related features within the SSM module, offering a more direct and expressive adaptation strategy. Specifically, they introduce "State-offset Tuning," which adds a learnable state-offset to the hidden state at each time step, ensuring a consistent effect across timesteps.  The paper presents experimental results on various datasets, demonstrating that State-offset Tuning outperforms existing fine-tuning techniques and can achieve performance comparable to full fine-tuning with significantly fewer parameters. The authors further explore connections to iterative suffix-tuning and low-rank adaptations to the state-offset.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper introduces a novel and well-motivated PEFT technique (State-offset Tuning) specifically designed for SSMs. The insight that prompt-based methods are sub-optimal due to SSM's internal state dynamics is a solid foundation. The connection to iterative suffix-tuning provides further justification for the approach. Low-rank adaptations are well established PEFT solutions, but the application is relevant here to show the efficacy of these models for SSMs.

*   **Significance:** The work addresses an important gap in PEFT research. As SSMs gain traction as alternatives to Transformers, effective and efficient fine-tuning strategies become crucial. State-offset Tuning provides a practical solution for adapting SSMs to downstream tasks with limited computational resources. The improved performance compared to existing PEFT methods showcases the potential of state-based tuning.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   Clear problem definition and motivation.
    *   Well-defined state-based PEFT framework.
    *   Novel State-offset Tuning method with strong empirical results across diverse datasets.
    *   Theoretical connection to iterative suffix-tuning.
    *   Thorough experimental evaluation and comparisons to relevant baselines, including LoRA, BitFit, and prompt-based techniques.
    *   Analysis of training speed and memory usage demonstrating the efficiency of State-offset Tuning.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   Limited exploration of different SSM architectures: The paper primarily focuses on Mamba, specifically its S6 component. While this is a reasonable starting point, the generalizability of State-offset Tuning to other SSM architectures could be explored further.
    *   Limited theoretical analysis: While the connection to iterative suffix-tuning is discussed, a deeper theoretical analysis of why State-offset Tuning is more effective for SSMs would strengthen the paper. Further study of the effect of training speed, memory usage, model size, and dataset size.
    *   Lack of ablations: While the approach does introduce low-rank factorizations as an ablation, it only further explores low-rank adaptions to the State-offset Tuning. A more thorough dissection of design choices in the tuning approach could further reinforce the advantages of State-offset Tuning.

*   **Potential Impact:** The paper has the potential to significantly impact the field of SSM research.  It provides a practical and effective PEFT technique that can facilitate the wider adoption of SSMs in resource-constrained environments. The state-based PEFT framework could inspire further research into methods that leverage the unique architectural properties of SSMs.

**Justification for Score:**

The paper offers a novel approach to PEFT specifically tailored for State Space Models, showing notable empirical improvements and a clear rationale. While there are weaknesses in the depth of theoretical analysis and exploration of the generalizability to other SSM architectures, the work addresses a relevant problem with a well-defined solution and solid empirical validation. The potential impact on enabling more efficient and widespread use of SSMs warrants a high score.

Score: 8

- **Classification**: cs.LG
- **Score**: 8/10

### CURVALID: Geometrically-guided Adversarial Prompt Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03502v1)
- **Authors**: Canaan Yung, Hanxun Huang, Sarah Monazam Erfani, Christopher Leckie
- **Abstract**: Adversarial prompts capable of jailbreaking large language models (LLMs) and inducing undesirable behaviours pose a significant obstacle to their safe deployment. Current mitigation strategies rely on activating built-in defence mechanisms or fine-tuning the LLMs, but the fundamental distinctions between adversarial and benign prompts are yet to be understood. In this work, we introduce CurvaLID, a novel defense framework that efficiently detects adversarial prompts by leveraging their geometric properties. It is agnostic to the type of LLM, offering a unified detection framework across diverse adversarial prompts and LLM architectures. CurvaLID builds on the geometric analysis of text prompts to uncover their underlying differences. We theoretically extend the concept of curvature via the Whewell equation into an $n$-dimensional word embedding space, enabling us to quantify local geometric properties, including semantic shifts and curvature in the underlying manifolds. Additionally, we employ Local Intrinsic Dimensionality (LID) to capture geometric features of text prompts within adversarial subspaces. Our findings reveal that adversarial prompts differ fundamentally from benign prompts in terms of their geometric characteristics. Our results demonstrate that CurvaLID delivers superior detection and rejection of adversarial queries, paving the way for safer LLM deployment. The source code can be found at https://github.com/Cancanxxx/CurvaLID
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the paper "CURVALID: Geometrically-guided Adversarial Prompt Detection":

**Summary:**

The paper introduces CurvaLID, a novel, model-agnostic framework for detecting adversarial prompts in Large Language Models (LLMs). CurvaLID leverages geometric properties of text prompts, specifically local intrinsic dimensionality (LID) at the sentence level and a curvature measure (TextCurv) at the word level, to distinguish adversarial prompts from benign ones. The framework operates independently of the internal architecture of the target LLM, offering a unified defense across different LLMs and attack types. Experiments demonstrate CurvaLID's superior detection and rejection of adversarial queries, with high accuracy and computational efficiency compared to existing defense mechanisms.

**Critical Evaluation:**

**Strengths:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper's core contribution lies in its geometric approach to adversarial prompt detection. Existing defenses primarily rely on prompt engineering, adversarial training, or modifications based on LLM internals. Applying geometric concepts like curvature and local intrinsic dimensionality to analyze and differentiate prompts offers a fresh perspective.
*   **Model-Agnosticism:**  A significant strength is that CurvaLID is designed to be model-agnostic. This means it doesn't rely on specific architectural details of the LLM, allowing it to be used across various models without needing to be re-engineered. This generality addresses a key limitation of many current defense strategies.
*   **Comprehensive Geometric Analysis:** By combining sentence-level LID (PromptLID) and word-level curvature (TextCurv), the framework provides a more thorough analysis of prompt characteristics. Addressing the shortcomings of token-level LID by using prompt-level LID is a well-justified design choice. The extension of Whewell's equation to define TextCurv in a high-dimensional embedding space is theoretically grounded.
*   **Strong Experimental Results:** The paper presents extensive experimental results demonstrating CurvaLID's effectiveness. The evaluations cover a wide range of adversarial prompts and languages, highlighting its ability to detect and filter malicious inputs with high accuracy. It also shows superior detection of adversarial prompts over several SOTA solutions. The ablation studies are well-designed, testing CurvaLID's performance under varying conditions (e.g., less training data, different embedding models).
*   **Computational Efficiency:**  The paper emphasizes the computational advantages of CurvaLID, particularly in terms of training time. The fact that it requires significantly less GPU time than adversarial training methods is a crucial practical benefit.

**Weaknesses:**

*   **Limited Theoretical Depth:** While the paper provides theoretical justification for TextCurv by extending Whewell's equation, the geometric interpretation of PromptLID could be further elaborated upon. A deeper understanding of *why* certain geometric features are indicative of adversarial prompts could strengthen the framework.
*   **Dependence on Pre-trained CNN:**  CurvaLID relies on a CNN trained to classify benign prompts, which could potentially introduce biases if the benign datasets are not representative of the real-world prompts the system will encounter. The potential for this bias to affect performance is not sufficiently explored.
*   **Generalizability Concerns:** Although CurvaLID demonstrates strong performance across several LLMs, more evidence is needed to definitively prove that it will generalize effectively to *any* LLM, including those with radically different architectures or training regimes that differ significantly from existing models.
*   **Potential for Circumvention:** While the geometric approach is novel, adversarial actors may attempt to develop prompts that are specifically designed to evade geometric detection by mimicking the geometric properties of benign prompts. The robustness of CurvaLID against such adaptive attacks should be investigated.
*   **No Real-World Deployment:**  The paper lacks an evaluation of CurvaLID in a real-world deployment scenario. Performance in a controlled lab setting might not fully translate to operational performance given the challenges of noise in real-world prompts.

**Significance:**

The paper has the potential to significantly influence the field of LLM security. Its model-agnostic design and geometric analysis provides a novel and promising alternative to existing defense mechanisms. However, to fully realize this potential, future research should focus on addressing the limitations mentioned above (e.g., improving the theoretical depth, mitigating potential biases, and evaluating real-world performance).

**Justification for Score:**

Considering the strengths and weaknesses, the paper's novelty is significant but the lack of an analysis on its generalizability beyond the evaluated models and its reliance on a CNN model that is specific for benign prompts limits the impact in real-world environments.

Score: 7

**Rigorous Rationale:**

A score of 7 reflects the paper's solid contribution to adversarial prompt detection. The geometric approach is innovative and backed by strong empirical results. However, several concerns including its dependence on a static CNN model, its real-world reliability and deployment, and theoretical limitations need to be addressed before it can be considered a truly groundbreaking work. A score of 8 or higher would require more conclusive evidence of robustness and real-world applicability.

- **Classification**: cs.CL
- **Score**: 7/10

### NeuGrasp: Generalizable Neural Surface Reconstruction with Background Priors for Material-Agnostic Object Grasp Detection
- **Link**: [Link to Paper](http://arxiv.org/abs/2503.03511v1)
- **Authors**: Qingyu Fan, Yinghao Cai, Chao Li, Wenzhe He, Xudong Zheng, Tao Lu, Bin Liang, Shuo Wang
- **Abstract**: Robotic grasping in scenes with transparent and specular objects presents great challenges for methods relying on accurate depth information. In this paper, we introduce NeuGrasp, a neural surface reconstruction method that leverages background priors for material-agnostic grasp detection. NeuGrasp integrates transformers and global prior volumes to aggregate multi-view features with spatial encoding, enabling robust surface reconstruction in narrow and sparse viewing conditions. By focusing on foreground objects through residual feature enhancement and refining spatial perception with an occupancy-prior volume, NeuGrasp excels in handling objects with transparent and specular surfaces. Extensive experiments in both simulated and real-world scenarios show that NeuGrasp outperforms state-of-the-art methods in grasping while maintaining comparable reconstruction quality. More details are available at https://neugrasp.github.io/.
- **Summary**: Here's a summary and critical evaluation of the NeuGrasp paper:

**Summary:**

The paper introduces NeuGrasp, a novel neural surface reconstruction method for robotic grasping, specifically addressing challenges posed by transparent and specular objects. NeuGrasp leverages background priors within a neural implicit surface framework. It integrates transformers and global prior volumes to robustly reconstruct scenes from sparse viewpoints.  The method enhances foreground object attention, uses an occupancy-prior volume for improved spatial perception, and demonstrates superior grasping performance on transparent/specular objects compared to existing methods, without relying on explicit depth supervision. Both surface reconstruction and grasp detection are trained end-to-end.  The paper demonstrates the effectiveness of NeuGrasp in both simulated and real-world scenarios.

**Critical Evaluation:**

*   **Novelty:** The paper presents several novel aspects:
    *   **Background Prior Integration:** Utilizing background priors within a NeRF-based grasping framework to handle transparent and specular objects is a significant contribution. This is a well-motivated approach, given the difficulties traditional depth sensors have with such materials.
    *   **Residual Feature Enhancement:** The proposed module to enhance foreground object attention by contrasting features from scene and background images is novel.
    *   **Occupancy-Prior Volume:** Using a global implicit occupancy derived from residual features to enhance spatial perception, especially with transparent objects, is a strong contribution.

*   **Significance:**

    *   **Addressing a Critical Problem:** Robotic grasping of transparent and specular objects is a long-standing challenge. NeuGrasp offers a promising solution that doesn't require direct depth information, making it potentially more robust in real-world scenarios.
    *   **Generalizability:**  The method emphasizes generalizability, avoiding per-scene optimization or dense view requirements, which are limitations of some prior works like DexNeRF. This aligns well with the need for deployable robotic systems.
    *   **End-to-End Training:**  The end-to-end training approach streamlines the pipeline and allows for efficient optimization of both reconstruction and grasping.
    *   **Real-time Performance:** The method runs in near real-time (0.27s), enabling its potential applicability on real robots for dynamic grasping tasks.

*   **Strengths:**
    *   **Strong Performance:**  The experimental results clearly demonstrate the superiority of NeuGrasp over existing methods, particularly in scenarios with transparent and specular objects. The ablation studies effectively highlight the contribution of each component.
    *   **Clear Methodology:**  The paper presents a well-defined and clearly explained methodology.
    *   **Comprehensive Evaluation:** The method is evaluated in both simulated and real-world environments.
    *   **Addressing limitations of prior methods:** The paper convincingly shows how NeuGrasp improves on the limitations of other NeRF-based grasping approaches.
    *   **Improved robustness of the geometric reconstruction.** Qualitative and quantitative results show an improvement upon other methods.

*   **Weaknesses:**
    *   **Dependency on Static Backgrounds:** While effective, the method relies on static background priors. Changes in the background could negatively impact performance.  The paper could have included a discussion of the sensitivity to such changes and potential mitigation strategies.
    *   **Limited Real-World Evaluation:** The real-world experiments are relatively small-scale, and while promising, more extensive evaluation would strengthen the claims.
    *   **Limited discussion about the need for fine-tuning in different datasets.** The paper could explore the need for transfer learning, if the robot is deployed in a different scenario or with a different object-set.
    *   **The object set in the simulated experiments is relatively simple.** Conducting experiments with more complex objects, could highlight limitations of the method.

*   **Potential Influence:**  NeuGrasp has the potential to influence research in robotic grasping, neural surface reconstruction, and material-agnostic perception. The integration of background priors and transformer-based architectures offers a compelling approach for dealing with challenging object properties.

**Justification for Score:**

NeuGrasp addresses a significant problem in robotic grasping with a novel and well-executed approach. While the dependency on static backgrounds and the limited real-world evaluation are minor weaknesses, the overall contribution is substantial. The paper demonstrates clear improvements over state-of-the-art methods and presents a generalizable and efficient solution for grasping transparent and specular objects. The approach could have been strengthened by an analysis of robustness when the assumption of a fixed background is violated and further information about the dataset and fine-tuning is needed.
